Gold supporting: it has been previously shown that disruption of rad26 in yeast strain w303 - 1b results in a strain that is deficient in transcription - coupled repair ( tcr ), the preferential repair of the transcribed strand of an expressed gene over the non - transcribed strand and the rest of the genome.
LLM supporting:  the provided context does not directly address the specific dna strand targeted by transcription - coupled repair ( tcr ). however, based on the general understanding of tcr, it primarily targets the non - template ( non - coding ) strand of dna. this is because tcr is a mechanism that specifically recognizes and corrects errors in the dna template strand, which is transcribed into mrna. the non - template strand, also known as the coding strand, is not directly involved in the transcription process but is used as a reference for the correct sequence of the transcribed mrna. therefore, when a mutation or error occurs in the dna template strand, tcr can identify and correct it, ensuring that the resulting mrna is accurate. this targeted correction of the non - template strand is crucial for maintaining the integrity of gene expression and preventing the propagation of mutations.

LLM supporting:  bach1 is a repressor. in the context of heme oxygenase - 1 ( ho - 1 ) gene regulation, bach1 acts as a repressor that blocks the access of transcriptional activators, such as the nrf2 / maf heterodimer, to the ho - 1 promoter. this repression is relieved in the presence of heme, which binds to bach1 and prevents it from inhibiting the activators, thereby allowing for the induction of ho - 1 expression. the role of bach1 as a repressor is well - established through gene targeting experiments in mice, where its absence leads to constitutive, high - level expression of ho - 1 under normal physiological conditions. additionally, chromatin immunoprecipitation studies have shown that small maf proteins, which are part of the nrf2 / maf heterodimer, can both repress and activate the ho - 1 gene, further supporting the antagonistic relationship between bach1 and nrf2.

Gold supporting: chronic myeloid leukaemia ( cml ) is a genetically associated malignancy of haematopoietic stem cells, characterized by a t ( 9 ; 22 ) translocation that forms the philadelphia chromosome and creates a novel fusion gene, bcr - abl.
LLM supporting:  the tyrosine kinase involved in philadelphia chromosome positive chronic myelogenous leukemia ( cml ) that is targeted by imatinib ( gleevec ) is the bcr - abl1 tyrosine kinase. this conclusion is based on the well - established molecular mechanism of action of imatinib, which specifically targets and inhibits the bcr - abl1 tyrosine kinase, a key driver of cml. the bcr - abl1 fusion protein, resulting from the translocation between chromosomes 9 and 22, is a critical factor in the uncontrolled cell proliferation seen in cml. imatinib's ability to block the signaling pathways mediated by bcr - abl1 makes it an effective therapeutic agent for cml.
